## 3. Per floor (07 §8.2)

*Every arrival on a floor by every recipe of that party size, all death rules pooled; "arrivals" counts the delves that stood on the floor. The band is the floor's threat chip for the party on arrival (D-092). Gold is what was found on the floor; its target is `07` §2.3's.*

- **LOW** the pair, ready (L14, C gear) · Nightmare · normal · textbook: party ambushed, % of fights: 7.5% (target 10–30%)
- **LOW** classic four, fresh, diving into the Crypts (L1) · Mortal · normal · textbook: party ambushed, % of fights: 4.8% (target 10–30%)
- **LOW** classic four, from the Cellars, diving into the Vault (L5, E gear) · Mortal · normal · textbook: party ambushed, % of fights: 4.3% (target 10–30%)

## 12. Reading the report

- Targets are data: tuning.json → `runner.targets` and each recipe's `clearTarget` (D-084's for the Cellars; the Crypts' and the Vault's carried over, Proposed). Only Normal without Pacts is judged; Hard, Dread and Pacts are reported.
- A **fight** is 3–6 rounds for ordinary packs (03 §3.6), elites 6–8, bosses 10–15, hunters 6–8.
- Gold per cleared delve should land near **R** at the delve's band (03 §8.1; 0.7–1.3 R), with the full repair bill 15–35% of R; a fully used weapon should lose 25–35% per delve (08 §9.4); the party should be surprised in 10–30% of fights (03 §4.4).
- **Deaths** are falls past the revive window: under Mortal and Nightmare each is a dead hero (D-088); they are reported until James sets targets.
- **Stuck** means the scripted player found no legal way on (sim/src/policies.ts never wanders); with **capped** (the command cap) it should never happen.
- A hook the sim has no joint for logs `hookStub` (sim/src/skills/custom/README.md) and §10 lists the ones that fired.

## 13. What changed, why, and what is left (hand-written by pass F after the run)

*Round 2 (`2026-10-28-gravehollow-round2.md`) acts on §13.3: the Vault judged as F7 and a start on F8, the boss row (D-128), the back-row targeting and the deeper loot tables' gold; its §13 supersedes the numbers below.*

### 13.0 The verdict

*The textbook player, Normal, Mortal (the game James asked for), 100 seeds a recipe; bosses meet the party for the second time (repeat kills, `runner.bossKills`).*

| Zone | Inside its target | Outside |
|---|---|---|
| **The Cellars** (F1–3) | fresh solo 0% · glass cannons 22% · levelled crypt-breakers 95% · levelled pair 75% | **high:** the classic four 84% (40–75) · fresh crypt-breakers 72% (15–55) · the fresh pair 41% (5–40); **low:** the ground solo (L12, D) 33% (40–85) |
| **The Crypts** (F4–6) | arriving solo 0% · the classic four 53% · crypt-breakers 29% · glass cannons 1% | **low:** the ground solo (L16, C) 21% · the arriving pair 2% · ready crypt-breakers (L9, D) 51% (70–100) · the ready pair 11% |
| **The Drowned Vault** (F7–8, two bosses) | arriving solo 0% · glass cannons 1% | **low:** everything else — the classic four 25% (40–75) · crypt-breakers 1% · ready crypt-breakers (L14, C) 13% · the ground solo (L21, B) 0% |

- **The done-when** (`12` Phase 7: "a solo hero and a 4-hero party can clear floors 1–8 on Normal … the runner's judgement lines inside targets"): a four clears every zone (84% · 53% · 25%); a ground solo clears the Cellars (33%) and the Crypts (21%) but not the Vault (0%). The judgement lines are not yet inside targets: 233 OK, 156 LOW, 162 HIGH (§11). The systemic causes are below (§13.3); the numbers are the best a per-boss tune reaches before the structural fixes proposed there.
- **What holds** (§1, §9, §11): no delve stuck or capped in 11,920; deaths per floor arrival climb with the threat band — Even 0.69, Dangerous 1.03, Deadly 1.59 (D-092); the careful player dies less than the textbook one (1.09 vs 1.35 deaths a delve) and walks out of every dive at once with no deaths; pass A's class-damage spread is 1.4× · 1.3× · 1.5× · 1.5× at L1 · 5 · 8 · 13 (the last just over 1.5×).
- **The dive set** (D-092 "too far"): a fresh classic four (L1) sent into the Crypts clears 32% of the time and loses 1.68 heroes a delve — the level-gap XP (D-096) levels it fast on the way down; into the Vault at L5 it clears 3% and wipes 67%.
- **Hard and Dread** (spot checks, 40 seeds): Hard roughly halves the Normal clears (the levelled crypt-breakers 65%, the classic four 8%); **Dread clears nothing at these recipes' levels** — it unlocks after a final boss on Hard (07 §6.1) and needs a higher-level reference party before it can be judged.
- **Where heroes die** (§4b): 74% of all deaths in boss fights (42% of boss fights cost a hero); ordinary fights 1%. The deadliest: Naesa with a Deep Tentacle risen (0.95 deaths a fight), the Barrow Warden with his Hound at heel (0.91), Vexa (0.29), the King's last phase (1.61, few fights). Behind every boss the **back row goes unhit**: in 76–91% of boss fights nobody touches the back-row adds, which deal 12–39% of their side's damage (§4).

### 13.1 Rules that changed since the checkpoint-1 reports (rule fixes, not tuning drift)

The checkpoint-1 reports (`2026-10-28-checkpoint1-mortal.md`, `…-nightmare.md`) were measured on rules that Phase 7 has since fixed, so a comparison with them is a rules change. Their tuning (Vexa ×1.6/×14 above all) assumed the collapsed behaviour.

- **Traps fire as 07 §2.5 says** (pass C1): only a revealed trap blocks its door; an unrevealed one fires on its trigger (a door opened, a chest, the stairs, gold taken). The Cellars' Tripwire Bell, Loose Flagstone, Tar Pot and Rat Cage Drop now fire — the last is a Plague Rat swarm whose bites stack Poison to its 5-stack cap at once (3% of max HP a stack a turn, ignoring Defense: 15% a turn whatever the hero's level).
- **Monster row targets** (C1): `partyFront`/`partyBack` land on the heroes' rows (the Broodmother's Sweep, the Plague Rat's death cloud).
- **Monster behaviour rolls its weights** (C1, C2): a trailing "always → X" gambit had made `06`'s aiWeights dead, so most Cellars monsters used one attack and Vexa only Grin (a Mark with no damage). Now Netters net, Weavers web, Old Gnaw lunges, and Vexa throws knives, re-calls her crew, shoots out lanterns and strikes twice under 20%.
- **Telegraphed attacks wind up one turn** (06 §8.10, C2) and Daze/Stun/Silence break them off.
- **Out of combat**: Ambush Sense and passive Detection count; Cartography's ring reveal exists and every Arcanist starts at rank 3. **Arrows are counted** (D); **set bonuses apply** (B).
- **D-127 (this pass; Proposed — 03 §3.5 "skip the next action (energy −1,000)", §3.6 "a round = every living hero has acted once")**:
  - a control push (Freeze, Stun, Daze, Petrify) lands once until the target's turn comes up; a later application still takes the status (its mark, duration and Freeze's ×1.5 next hit), not the push — tuning `combat.pushGuard`. Three Tidebound Acolytes chain-Froze a Warden for a whole fight before; the round counter stalled with him, so the tide (every 3 rounds) and the 30-round cap stopped and Naesa read *easier* (a four's "rounds to kill" was 2.6; now 10–11);
  - a Stun, Freeze or Petrify costs one action, as 03 §3.5 writes it: the data carried both the −1,000 push and a 100% skip, so each cost two (`skipsActionChance` 0 for the three; Fear keeps its 50%);
  - once every hero has fallen the fight is lost (03 §12.2 "All heroes fallen = wipe"): a pet left standing kept a fight going with no round to close and no cap — the first full run of this pass died of memory on such a fight (`cry-solo-ground`, Sheltered, seed 71). A downed hero still counts, so a pet may still win inside the revive window (§12.1).
- **The scripted players** (this pass): the textbook trap loop (Gravehollow F2, seed 101) is gone — every player walks the shortest way and clears a revealed trap that shuts it; a player with no legal way on ends the delve as *stuck* (none in this report); the most hurt hero drinks their own potion (full-HP heroes used to drink theirs); on a delve's last floor a hurt party **finishes** rather than teleports out (earlier reports counted a boss beaten and walked away from as "retreated", deflating clear rates); under the Iron Oath (no retreat) the players push on instead of pacing between the doors. They never sell, never use corridor skills and never buy arrows (a Ranger can run dry on a long delve: read the Ranger's numbers with that in mind).

### 13.2 Tuning changes in this pass (numbers only; each measured with `--set` what-ifs before it went into content)

| What | Where | Before | Now | The runner's evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Captain Vexa Marrow | `monsters/captain-vexa-marrow.json` multipliers | Attack ×1.6, HP ×14 | ×1.15, ×10 | With her real kit (no longer Grin only) no solo or pair beat her and the ground solo cleared 0% (D-084: 40–85%); at ×1.15/×10 six of the Cellars' eight targets land and the other two sit at their edges. |
| the Barrow Warden | `monsters/barrow-warden.json` multipliers | ×1.8, ×16 | ×1.2, ×10 | Under his Oath he hits little; his length is what lets his adds and his hound work. |
| the Warden's Hound | `monsters/wardens-hound.json` multipliers | ×1.8, ×7 | ×1.3, ×5 | 48% of its hunter fights cost a hero; Speed 130 and the Howl's party-wide Fear. |
| the Hound at the Warden's heel (`07` §2.8 "a second actor at 60% HP") | `barrow-warden.json` arena rule `heel` | 60% HP | 20% HP | Warden fights with it cost 0.95 deaths each, without it 0.17; the party never turned on it. |
| Sister Naesa | `monsters/sister-naesa.json` multipliers | ×1.7, ×15 | ×1.4, ×12 | At ×1.4/×12 the three- and four-hero recipes land F7; solos and pairs still fall. |
| the Hollow King (`07` Q22) | `monsters/hollow-king.json` multipliers | ×2.0, ×30 | ×1.0, ×7 | No party beat the throne at ×30, nor at ×16 with every softening of phase 3 tried; his HP sets how long phase 3's crowd has. |
| the returning hunters (the King's phase 3) | `tuning.json` → `arena.hunters` | 100% HP, the throne's level | 40% HP, two levels under | Phase 3 killed nearly every party that reached it; also sets the Warden's heel hound two levels under the floor. |
| a pair's monster HP | `tuning.json` → `dungeon.partyScaling.hp[1]` | ×1.0 | ×0.85 | Pairs failed bosses outright; at Vexa the fresh pair went 17 → 27% and the levelled pair 60 → 80%. 03 §11.2's own starting value (D-084 had raised it for pairs' ordinary fights). |
| the control push guard (D-127) | `tuning.json` → `combat.pushGuard` | — | on | §13.1. |
| Stun, Freeze, Petrify: the skipped turn | `statuses/*.json` `skipsActionChance` | 100 | 0 | §13.1: the push is the skipped action. |

Not changed, though tried: `dungeon.monsterBaseline.gold` (fight gold does not read it — §13.3); the Hound's Howl weight and Speed; the hunters' HP at 25%.

### 13.3 Still off target, and the smallest fix proposed

1. **The Drowned Vault** (Naesa on F7, the Hollow King on F8, one delve). Naesa falls to fours in 69% of her fights and to trios in 44%; the King to fours in 45% and trios in 20% — and a Vault clear needs both back to back, while under Mortal any death before the throne sends the party home. *Measured:* §1 and §4. *Proposed:* (a) the throne's King stays at ×7 HP (07 Q22) and the returning hunters at 40% HP and two levels under the throne; (b) the textbook player turns on a back-row add that outdamages the boss (Naesa's Acolytes are never hit in most of her fights, §4) — a player fix (`sim/src/ai.ts`), not a number; (c) for James: a full heal at the throne floor's rest, or the zone's two bosses in two delves.
2. **Small parties against bosses.** A pair faces a trio's boss (×1.0 HP and damage) with two thirds of its damage, so each hero takes about 2.25× the punishment; a solo faces the boss's adds nearly unscaled. Pair HP scaling went 1.0 → 0.85 (03 §11.2's own starting value), which lands the Cellars pairs; deeper, pairs and ground solos stay short. *Proposed (formula, for checkpoint 2):* a boss row in `dungeon.partyScaling` (HP, damage and add count for 1–4 heroes, read by `spawnBossFight` and the arena's adds) so bosses scale on their own line while ordinary fights keep D-084's.
3. **Gold per fight** runs about 1.5–2× `08` §11's band from F3 on, and gold per cleared delve 1.3–2× R. Fight gold is the loot table's roll × Σ(the monsters' gold multipliers) ÷ 3 (`loot.ts` `encounterTypicalSize`): a four meets ×1.6 the monsters and the Crypts' and Vault's packs run 4–6, so Σ outgrows the typical three. *Proposed (formula):* divide by the party-scaled typical size (3 × the count scale), so a fight pays `08`'s band whatever the party size; or (numbers) the Crypts', Vault's and throne's `goldRange` down by a third. The boss purse is `08` §11's own row and counts in its floor's gold (`07` §2.3's floors sum to R). Untouched here: `loot.ts` and the loot tables are pass B's.
4. **Elite fights** run short in the Cellars (3–5 rounds against 6–8) and long for small parties in the Vault; one champion HP (×3, D-084) cannot fix both. *Proposed:* leave until the boss scaling above settles; then a champion HP by band if still needed.
5. **Hunters**: the Warden's Hound was the Crypts' deadliest fight (48% of its fights cost a hero) and, at the Barrow Warden's heel, the Crypts' wall (0.95 deaths a fight with it, 0.17 without — the party never turned on it). Tuned: ×1.3 attack, ×5 HP, at heel 20% HP and two levels under the floor.
6. **Poison swarms**: a Plague Rat swarm (the Rat Cage Drop) stacks Poison to 15% of max HP a turn at any level; trap fights are 2% of all deaths, so it is a flavour of danger, not a wall. Left as is.
7. **The scripted players never sell, use corridor skills or buy arrows**: the Ranger may run dry on long delves; the class-damage measure (§8b) is the fair read of its damage.

### 13.4 Proposed decisions from this pass (for `13`)

Each: the docs' words, the choice, the reason.

1. **D-127, control and rounds** (03 §3.5–§3.6, §12.2; the lead's D-127): a push lands once until the target's turn comes up; a Stun, Freeze or Petrify costs one action; a fight is lost once every hero has fallen. *Reason:* "skip the next action (energy −1,000)" is one action; a round "every living hero has acted once" must be able to close, or the arena's round clocks and the round cap stop.
2. **The careful scripted player** (12 Phase 7 "a careful player turns back from Deadly floors"; D-092): it turns back from a floor whose chip reads Deadly — its monsters, or its boss's chip (`runner.careful.bossCounts`) — at the stairs and on arriving, and never opens a door it knows reads Deadly; under the Iron Oath it cannot turn back and plays the textbook. *Reason:* the Waystone and the stairs show both chips.
3. **No wandering**: a scripted player with no legal way on stops; `runDelveAuto` ends the delve as capped, "Stuck on floor N". *Reason:* a wander to the command cap hid the F2 loop for a phase.
4. **The runner's recipes for the Crypts and the Vault** mirror D-084's Cellars eight at `07` §1.2's recommended levels (arriving L5/E and L11/D, ground L16/C and L21/B, ready L9/D and L14/C) with the Cellars' targets carried over. *Reason:* no doc sets deeper targets; D-084's structure is James's.
5. **Repeat kills**: the runner's delves meet bosses that have died before (08 §11's repeat rows; `--kills first` pays the first-kill lines). *Reason:* R is "the median gold one delve yields" (03 §8.1) — the steady state, not the once-per-save first kill.
6. **Tolerances**: gold per floor judged within ×0.6–1.4 of `07` §2.3; the class-damage spread at ≤ 1.5× (A's measure, damage dealers); weapon wear judged on the party's most-worn main hand for starter-kit parties (08 §9.4 "a fully used F weapon"). *Reason:* the docs give a target, not a band, and a caster's focus never wears from spells.
7. **Rule 64's zone step** (D-092 "floors climb in level faster than a party that dives without grinding"): each zone's first floor stands ≥ `deadlyFrom` levels above the previous zone's first floor. *Reason:* "too far" reads Deadly by construction.
8. **The tuning in §13.2** — every number a starting point (CLAUDE.md rule 3); the at-heel hound's 60% (`07` §2.8) and the King's ×30 (`06`, `07` Q22) are doc-stated numbers the runner moved.

### 13.5 Open questions for James (checkpoint 2)

- **07 Q22 — the King's first phase is long**: the runner's answer is his HP. At ×30 (and at ×16 with every throne softening tried) no party beat the throne; at ×7 a ready party wins some. Keep ×7 (the report's numbers), or keep more HP and soften the throne rule instead?
- **07 Q15 — the role mix often misses** (C1: on 300 seeds F2 misses `06` §8.2's mix 44% of the time, F5 54%, F7 36%). The runner sees no harm from it — ordinary fights take a hero in about 1% of fights — so re-rolling backwards is a matter of variety, not balance.
- **Small parties against bosses**: solos and pairs fall to bosses far more than their recipes' targets allow (§13.3). Should bosses scale with party size on their own line (Proposed: a `partyScaling.boss` row), or should a pair simply be weak at bosses ("best taken with a party", D-084)?
- **The Drowned Vault** is two bosses back to back; under Mortal any death sends the party home, so a clear needs two flawless boss fights. Keep, or give the throne floor's rest a full heal?
- **The back row**: behind Vexa's barricade and around Naesa only bows, spells and reach weapons hit the back row, and the textbook player rarely turns on it (§4). The smallest fix is the player's (target a back-row add that outdamages the boss); the next is 07 §2.8's barricade (2 kegs × 3 shots).
